the visualization of the deity, whatever it may be, is of two kinds, direct perception and inference.
Direct perception is through union with reality: many enjoyment bodies, like stars in the sky.
What is not direct is inference: whatever is imagined by what is not reality, like a corpse.
That is to be viewed in paintings and the like; it is the object of meditation for yogins of immature mind.

A form, or else maṇḍalas, are first drawn, and on that image one meditates by what is not reality.
In space, through union with reality, one's own mind is seen whole and entire, lacking nothing,
for half a year or one year, through the power of the guru's certainty, until it becomes stable.
Then, through union with the mudrā, within some days it becomes truly unchanging.

From all the kings of tantra, this vajra word has definitively been spoken to yogins.
For the sake of ripening the childish, the Universal Lord in supreme compassion has hidden it.
Therefore, having discerned it, the yogin should meditate on it at each time of day and night:
for the sake of the siddhi of the mudrā, the progenitor of the supreme Jinas, indestructible Kālacakra.

This is the fourth chapter, on the means of accomplishment, from glorious Kālacakra, extracted from the glorious great Ādibuddha.

"The sādhana of the principal deity, together with the supreme Jinas, which you have taught, I understand a little.
Again I wish to hear, O guru of gods and humans, of the maṇḍala according to the divisions of the elements,
and of the six families of buddhas and yoginīs abiding in the vowels and consonants."
Having heard the words of Sucandra, the lord of the Jinas taught the maṇḍala and the rest through the divisions of the elements.

The pillars, the vajra garland, the earth maṇḍala and the walls are to be made with bones.
With flesh, blood, water and feces the colored powders are laid for the gods, for Yama, for Kubera and the others;
with bile and phlegm, the sun and likewise the hare-marked one; with the sinews, the lotuses;
the lines of the five enclosures with what is born of earth, water, fire, wind and space.

With the channel of time, the lotus of the principal; the moving wind, the fire maṇḍala and the rest, with skin and so on;
the twelve doors with the bodily apertures; the arrangement of jewel nature likewise with the garland of teeth;
in the eight charnel grounds, the abodes in the maṇḍala of the moving wheel, with the nails of the fingers;
with the body hairs, the vajra flames, abiding at the edge of the maṇḍala in the directions and intermediate directions.

Thus the many qualities of the supreme body vajra of the maṇḍala have definitively been taught.
The speech vajra, the forms of the deities, ā and the rest, kā and the rest, all of them through the Jina families.
The mind vajra of the six empties, abiding in threefold existence, is the form of the principal.
Likewise the gnosis vajra, destroyer of the fear of existence, is the nature of Viśvamātā.

The six classes, kā and the rest, are set out in order, and their families, in order,
are vajra, sword, jewel, wheel, the water-born lotus, and the curved knife: these belong to the Jinas.
Each class in turn is again divided by the five divisions of the supreme Jina,
and the divisions of the individual letters come from the substances earth, water, fire, wind and space.

First, the divisions of the empty are ṅa ña ṇa ma na, ṭhka, and the first vowel.
The divisions of wind are the letter i and gha jha ḍha bha dha śa; the divisions of fire are ṛ and the rest.
By the divisions of water come the letter u and va kha cha ṭha pha tha va; the divisions of earth are ḷ and the rest.
In this way the fivefold families of the supreme Jina divide the classes kā and the rest.

The uṣṇīṣa and the five empties; likewise the three families are the vajras of body, speech and mind.
a and the rest are the mudrā family; and all the classes ka and the rest, joined with ha ya ra va la,
those that illumine the world, with their vowels and joined with ha ya ra va la, are the worldly and the world-transcending.
They become all the lords of the vidyā-mantra holders; and the eight abide in the stacked classes ka and the rest.

The thirty letters ka and the rest, joined with ha ya ra va la, together with kṣa, are thirty-six.
Divided by the modifications of the vowels, the six multiplied by six become the lords of the wheel.
The body is single; the one with its retinue is certainly two, embraced by the wisdom consorts.
The fivefold becomes so through the five; with its retinue it is nine, the nature of the nine lords of the wheel.

Having the nature of the number of the vowels, thirteen, nine and the directions, they also have the nature of thirty-six.
By ya and the rest come all the door guardians; the families of the whole assembly come from the remaining vowels.
By ha and the rest they become the six wrathful ones, abiding in the directions of their own families, through the divisions of the wind vowel and the rest.
Through the divisions of the individual letters, the six multiplied by six are the yoginīs and the yoga tantras.

Likewise the individual letters, six multiplied by six, are the lords of the maṇḍala in the maṇḍala of the six.
The aggregates, elements, sense faculties and the rest, the families of the whole assembly, are to be purified through the divisions of the vowels.
By all the Jina families, the empty, the indestructible and the rest, the hundred deities are set in place,
from the uṣṇīṣa down to the lotus, in the uneven and even families, upon the seat at the center of the wheel.

Through the vajras the faces are divided, and the arms of the Lord of the Jinas through the divisions of the vowels.
Through the principles come the divisions of the mudrās, and further, likewise, through the body vajra and the rest.
The thirty-six yoga tantras have immeasurable qualities and the divisions of many letters;
bearing the names of the individual letters, in the even and uneven families, they belong to the goddesses and the gods.

The single seed, the single hero, of one taste with the dust, is embraced by the wisdom consorts.
Through the aggregates he is fivefold; blessed by the earth element and the rest, he is also ninefold.
Surrounded by the anus and the rest and by the eye and the rest, he also has the nature of twenty-five.
Likewise, with the teeth fully grown, through the teeth once more, he is the Lord, Ananta, the cakravartin.

From water, perfectly purified as purity: white like the hare-bearer (the moon), with the lotus as his emblem, Amitābha;
Mānaka, Lokeśvara, the goddess who travels by horse chariot, Māmakī and Rūpavajrā;
Raudrī, the Lord of the Waters, Gaṇapati, the brahmin nāgas, and the tiger-faced goddess together with the owl:
these the vajra-holder emanated from his glorious left face, through supreme compassion.

From wind, perfectly purified as Dharma: with a sword in his lotus hand, Amoghasiddhi, and Tārā;
Khagarbha, Sparśavajrā, Atibala together with Carcikā, and Śṛṅkhalā;
Nairṛti, the one called Vāyu, the śūdra nāgas, and likewise the dog-faced goddess together with the crow:
these the lord of the three realms of existence emanated from his eastern face, with a mind of loving-kindness.

From fire, purified as perfect enjoyment: like the rising sun, the jewel-holder, and Pāṇḍaravāsinī;
Kṣitigarbha, Jambhaka, Rasavajrā, Vārāhī, Ṣaṇmukha, and the god of fire;
the two kṣatriya lords of the nāgas, the radiant Bhṛkuṭī, and the pig-faced goddess together with the vulture:
these the Universal Lord emanated from his glorious right face, through supreme joy.

From earth, perfectly purified at the navel: like supreme gold, the Sage with a wheel in his hand;
Locanā, Viṣkambhin, the most supreme lord of men, Stambhaka, and Gandhavajrā;
Marīcī, Aindrī, Śakra together with the time-faced one, the vaiśya nāgas and all the rest:
these, beneficial to all beings, the Principal emanated from his western face, through equanimity.

From space, purified as the uṣṇīṣa: with the supreme vajra in his hand, Akṣobhya, and Dhātvīśvarī;
Uṣṇīṣa and Dharmadhātu, and, through the quality of space, Vajrapāṇi and the others;
and from the purified secret, those who bestow the equal bliss of all, Śabdavajrā and the rest as well,
abodes of many qualities above and below, were emanated as the cause of liberation for living beings.

On the petals of the directions are Locanā and the rest; on the petals of the intermediate directions stand skull cups filled with blood.
Upon the sun, left leg extended, trampling Īśvara (Śiva) together with Umā, like the clouds at the time of destruction,
bound with a garland of skulls, with faces as many as the oceans and arms as many as the suns, is Heruka,
wearing the bone ornaments, serpents, a tiger skin, and the fresh-flayed hide of the terrible lord of elephants.

Black, red, and stainless like the hare-marked one (the moon) are the bodies; outside the goddesses are three wheels.
There are the six vajras born of the wrathful ones, two-armed and single-faced, and likewise those born of the bhūtas.
At the gates the caṇḍālīs stand upon corpses, holding a curved knife and a skull cup in their hands, and so too in the intermediate directions.

The heroes, in their four hands, hold vajra, ḍamaru, bell and vajra khaṭvāṅga.

In the eight directions of glorious Meru, on the maṇḍalas of earth and water, are all the pīṭhas and upapīṭhas;
the kṣetras, the chandohas and the melāpakas; and the charnel grounds lie amid the maṇḍalas of fire and wind.
Likewise in the lands and cities, the deities dwelling in the directions and intermediate directions are to be known.
This is the Abhidhāna of the wisdom tantras, emanated by the guru of the three realms of existence from his eastern face.

At the heart is the wheel of nine parts; from the maṇḍala, half a portion: on moon, sun and lotus,
adorned with garlands of vajra pillars, is the great presiding deity of the aggregates and elements.
Outside, on the walls of the enclosure, the sense objects and sense faculties dwell on moon and sun seats.
The doorkeepers dwell on the sun; the other families of the assembly are on the projections all around.

For this and the like, the Yoga Tantra too, the Samāja, is taught in six aspects:
at the corners the wrathful ones, on the sides the sense objects and faculties, and Jambhala holding grain in his hand.
Speech, feet and hands, anus, bhaga, sun and moon are the nature of the channels of time.
Wisdom and method in their entirety, with three faces and six arms, were emanated from the right face.

At the heart the wheel becomes twice four; O lord of men, from the maṇḍala, a third portion.
Again the presiding deities of the aggregates and elements; on the other door panels, the eight beginning with Śabdavajrā.
In the maṇḍala lying outside, arranged in the ten directions and dwelling on suns, are the hosts of wrathful ones;
there, on the walls of the enclosure, are all the bodhisattvas, six multiplied by four.

The Māyājāla, of three divisions, with three faces and six arms, from the divisions of the deities' bodies,
all that it contains of conceptions and the rest, was emanated from the left face, by the power of the hearts of the foolish.
The tantra of yogic understanding, and here the twofold Great Saṃvara of the ḍākinīs,
with its six wheels and six families, O lord of men, together with the charnel grounds, was emanated from the western face.

Having laid out the lines of the maṇḍala with six lines and nine portions, by those dwelling in the maṇḍala of wind,
from the heart, by portions of six and six, a lotus with half-portion petals and a hub as well;
curved knife, wheel, lotus, jewel, sword and supreme vajra, the garlands, each by two portions;
by twelve, the doors and porticoes; by six and six, the projections and the ground of the garland festoons;

and by three, the lines of the five walls; by three, the strip at the base of the festoons.
By twelve, the sides and borders; by three times twelve, the archways above the pillars.
In the directions and intermediate directions one should make eight doors; this great maṇḍala is round.
In the center is a multicolored lotus, and on its hub a seat of sun and moon joined.

The wheel and garland are blue, the ground green; on a black ground a yellow wheel;
on a red ground a white wheel; and on a white ground a red wheel together with its garland;
and the grounds yellow and blue, with wheels not white, and of green color.
By six portions is the ground of the weapons; and likewise, by the power of the generating face, the wheel of the doors.

Outside are the twice eight charnel grounds; likewise, by the power of the families, the eight goddesses of the heart.
On the petals are eight skull cups filled with the taste of nectar, and on the eight petals the goddesses.
Outside, the goddesses beginning with Lāsyā dwell in the directions and intermediate directions, and likewise the eight nāga kings.
Outside of that are the maṇḍalas of earth, water, fire and wind, and the vajra garland.

With long and short vowels as well, the letters sa ta pa ṭa ca ka, those of earth and the rest, and of space and the rest:
on the spokes of wind and the rest, by the movement of the sun, the long ones, the nature of the elements beginning with earth;
on the spokes of above and the rest, by the movement of the moon, the short ones, upon the spokes of their own wheels.
Between door and door are the classes dwelling on the floor of space, together with ha kṣa.

The ḍākinīs are dwarfish in form, with faces like the hare-bearer (the moon), their hands holding curved knife and skull cup,
the right leg extended, drinking asura blood from their own vessels, hair loosened, naked;
on feet, waist, forehead, ears, neck and hands, small bells and the like, and the bone ornaments;
on the head and at the sides of the waist, pieces of skull, and garlands of the five colors of the Jinas.

The ḍākas are two-faced, holding in their hands the supreme vajra, ḍamaru, vajra khaṭvāṅga and bell;
at the neck a glorious garland of heads, and on the head a crown with vajra, silk streamers and jewel;
a glorious necklace, anklets on the feet, bracelets on the arms, belts and earrings;
garlands of whole skulls; all of them fanged, and on the body the seal of ash.

On the head of the principal, a garland of skulls; at the neck, a garland of heads with a hundred faces;
a crescent moon and vajra upon the crown; at his waist and in his hands, tiger skin and elephant hide.
On the maṇḍalas of moon, sun and fire, beneath his feet, Māra and Īśvara (Śiva); on his body, ash;
sealed with the lords of nāgas, like Agni the Crested One at the time of destruction, he kisses the ḍākinī.

On the directional petals the ḍākinīs hold in their hands ḍamaru and vajra khaṭvāṅga;
on the intermediate petals they dwell with feces, urine, blood and human flesh, and hold vessels of semen in their hands as well.
Likewise the eight goddesses all around form a wheel absorbed in union in the bliss of both.
Yellow, white and red in color, black, green-bodied and blue in color, each in her own direction.

In the family of the Jina who generates them, the colors and marks of the principal are as before, with the hosts of three eyes.
Their mutual embraces too are to be understood all around by the power of their own and other families.

…and by generosity and the rest, by the aggregates, the elements, objects and subjects, and also by the five faculties and so forth,
by the directions, the grounds, the powers of the quarters, and the glorious lords, the Jina's sons, the goddesses too are to be purified.

In the regions of Meru, divided eightfold, in the directions and the intermediate directions, abide all the sacred sites and the secondary sites,
the fields, the chandohas, the meeting places, the charnel grounds, the earth and the dwellings, at the boundary of half the wind.
These are the buddha grounds: the Sun, the subtle, the body-born, humans and bhūtas, gods and asuras,
power and the principal one; in a single day the Kulikā goddesses roam, through the progression of sun and moon.

Behind is the goddess called Subduing Dissolution; here in front, by those whose nature is the five elements:
wind and Īśāna, asura, fire, the underworld and space, god and Yama, yakṣa, and also in the west.
They abide at the crown protuberance, in the region of the heart, at the throat and the head, at the navel and the secret wheel:
wheels of eight spokes, below and above, from the Makara of each day and night, two by two, transition by transition.

Through the principal's lotus, all the vajras and the immutable drops abiding in the vajra jewel,
joy on every side, all of it, is the abode of the vajra-holder's equal bliss, and its time is all times.
The moon melted in the guru's mouth: whatever has entered there is Kālacakra itself.
These are the wheels of the ḍākinīs, spoken of as the time of the Māras, the terror of death.

With the syllables of wisdom and means, through the power of both families, the names of the ḍākinīs and ḍākas,
with the three vajras and together with the other quarters, divided into beginning, middle and end, are to be recited.
Those blessed by prāṇa, through the power of the course of both, accord each with their own action;
the ḍākinīs and vajra ḍākas who dwell in the six realms of beings will swiftly be accomplished.

Those breaths which, at the time of arising in the central channel, together with their quarters, have become taste and sweetness,
by those eight become the wheel; through the power of the underworld and space, the goddesses abiding in the middle of the gates,
born from a single quarter, are the mothers of the three realms of existence, holding in their hands the curved knife and the skull cup.
Embracing with the portion of nectar's bliss, upon sun and moon, right leg extended: this is Kālacakra.

Through the power of five, three, the flames, the objects and the qualities, the seats of moon and sun come from the quarters of the Lord of the Waters;
the seven planets and the complete one, the Sun, the day-maker, and the hare-marked one: the seats are the others.
Of the dark and the bright, the complete one, the Sun, the day-maker, and the hare-marked one: the seats are the others,
and in this very one, the planets of the mountain abide in even and odd places: these are the seats of sun and moon.

The quarters of moon and sun, the spokes and the lotus petals, the four gates, the six of the corners;
again the quarters doubled: these are the very seats of the day-maker and the hare-marked one.
The complete planets and the Sun's quarters abide in even and odd places; the quarters of the Lord of the Waters are the seats.
Upon the sun or upon the full moon, by the power of the family, the principal one, or else the couple in union.

For wind there is one face of touch, and by the power of the other quality the sealing face is the second.
For Agni the Crested One the faces of form and touch become two, and the sealing face is the third.
For water the faces are touch, form and taste, and the sealing face is the fourth.
For earth the faces are the qualities from smell up to touch, and the sealing face is the fifth.

For space the faces are the five emptinesses, and the face that seals is the sixth.
Likewise it becomes the wisdom element: through the power of the qualities, the faces of the six emptinesses.
One mixed with four, they bestow the result of equal bliss; two and three mixed make five.
Wisdom and means are ten faces; through the power of the three qualities, twelve faces, in four ways.

By the division of the junctures of the watches they are fourfold; the threefold faces, by the division of the transitions, are of three colors.
The half-transition is the division of the arms, uneven and even; the embraces too, in body and in mind,
come from the earlier and later portions of the junctures: three faces, six arms embracing, in the portion of the middle.
Union with wisdom and absorption, and the purposes and the rest, belong to the manifest juncture of the watch.

On the red and yellow limbs of wisdom the intention of Kālacakra surely enters;
on the left and front limbs of the vajra-holder, white and like a dark cloud, is the intention of wisdom.
On the supreme body, divided by wisdom's intention, the left and front limbs become peaceful;
the wrathful right and rear limbs, divided by the intention of the supreme Lord of the Jinas, are likewise.

Divided by wisdom, the most supreme families of the hosts of Jinas become of twofold nature.
Divided by the principal of wisdom, in the intermediate directions too there is surely the host of yoginīs.
Right and not right are the paired bodies of the Sun and the hare-marked one, and so too east and west;
black and yellow, and likewise blue and green, abide in space and in the underworld.

For the white ones wisdom is red; for the red ones, white like the hare-bearer, likewise;
for the yellow ones the color is black; for the non-white families, like supreme gold;
for the blue ones the color is variegated; and again for the green ones, blue: so it is taught.
Likewise the goddesses are surely to be known, by their own families, in the gods abiding in the directions.

The wisdom tantra is from the east; and again the Anuyoga is from the western face.
From the right face the Lord of the Jinas taught the yoga tantra; from the left face, kriyā and the rest.
From the east, Yogācāra; and again from the western face, surely the whole of the Middle Way;
the Sautrāntika was taught from the right face; from the white face, the pure Vaibhāṣika.

From the western face the Ṛgveda, and from the left face the Lord of the Jinas taught the Yajurveda;
from the right face the Sāmaveda; and the supreme, for the class of burnt offerings, the guardian of existence, the Atharvaveda, from the eastern face.

From the eastern face came the tantra of the Kaulas, and again from the western face the tantras of the garuḍas and the bhūtas.
From the left face came the Śaiva doctrine; from the right face, the one like the rising sun, came the Vaiṣṇava teaching.
From the back, turning at once, from the supremely white face came the gods of the left hand and the rites of consecration.
From the right came the lore that is not Raudra, and again from the face of wind came the wind-born, Brahmā and the peaceful ones.
From the face of space came emptiness and the Lords. These the Lord of the three realms emanated for the sake of worldly people.
The Kṛta, Tretā and Dvāpara ages, and the other, the Kali age, come from the division into the back face and the rest.

Likewise the sequence of the heroes and so forth, and by the course of the sun the four junctures of the day;
foods from the right face, and the supremely terrifying ones were emanated from the back face.
From the front face came sexual union; from the supremely white face, kings, dreams and sleep.
From the back the kṣatriyas, the twice-born, the lords of men, and likewise the śūdras arise in sequence.

The Lord of the Jinas taught the Sarvāstivāda from the front, and from the right the Sāṃmitīya.
From the back face came the Sthaviras, and again the Mahāsāṅghikas from the left face.
From the front face humans, and again from the back face animals were emanated.
From the right face bhūtas and gods; from the supremely white face the hell beings and the asuras.

Those emanated from the front face arise from the heart cakra; from the back face, from the navel cakra;
from the right face, from the cakra of enjoyment; and from the left face, always from the cakra of the innate.
From the center of the faces of the cakras, the projection of things into the body and their withdrawal take place.
The entire three worlds, too, are rightly so through the divisions of the faces of the one who gives birth to the Jinas.

From the glorious moon, from the moonstone, whose nature is free of conceptual thought, water flows forth.
Likewise in the world, relying on the aggregates of the Lord of the Jinas, the Dharma of the Omniscient One trickles forth.
Just as water, depending on the seeds and elements, takes on a matching taste, so the dharmas,
through the pure minds of sentient beings, become manifold, owing to the force of past karma.

Dwelling in the three realms, the one who accomplishes the aims of beings, like the wish-fulfilling jewel, has no thought.
Abiding in the mind, it is not seen by beings of evil karma, because of the fruits of nonvirtue.
This is not the fault of the wish-fulfilling jewel: all beings fully experience the fruits of nonvirtue and virtue.
If the mind is pure, that very person becomes a Lord of the Jinas. What could another Jina do?

That which produces and gives rise to this sprout perishes once the thing has arisen.
Through that perishing the sprout fully arises, and again, O kings, it becomes the producer of seeds.
The former is called "dead" in relation to another thing; it is not born by its own nature.
Through that moment of bliss which has come from emptiness down to earth, one goes to the ten powers.

In this moment of attachment from bliss to bliss, the innate is not attached to any other thing.
The aggregates that it produced are produced again by that moment of bliss.
From the pure comes the dharma; other than that, and again other than that, and from that, the utterly pure;
just as roots, leaves, flowers and fruits come from a seed planted in pure ground.

Earth, water, fire, wind, taste and supreme taste: the atoms are of six kinds.
Each lacks one more of smell and the rest; the last, free of objects, is gnosis: these are what is to be seen.
The desire, form and formless realms are the pairs, the pairs, and the hare-marked one (the moon); the last is the dharmadhātu.
Possessing all aspects, these are wholly and always the basis of unchanging, innate bliss.

Again, in the earth and other elements, the lesser things abide mutually within the principal things.
Mutually the aggregates and elements, objects and subjects; mutually Māra and ignorance.
Mutually the five attachments abide in the courses of taste, of the moments of suffering and of bliss.
In all those of every aspect, wholly, the indivisible vajra that does not delight in other qualities truly abides.

Their Kālacakra, the emanation body which brings forth many great miracles that greatly illuminate,
by blazing, radiating vajras teaches the asuras and humans who dwell in the desire realm;
by the sambhogakāya, those who have form; and by the dharmakāya, the arhats in space, the sons of the Jinas and the rest.
By the part of emptiness, the entire three realms are the totality of space; by the winds, the totality of wind;

by the portions of fire, the totality of fire; by the portions of water emanated to beings, water too;
by the portions of the bearer, the totality of earth; and by the objects of the aggregate, the whole nature of things.
This single self, wholly equal to space, is shown on the pure ground.
Thus it becomes the body of a buddha, and because its nature is bliss alone, it does not die.

From that one alone come five aspects, and this complete and perfect awakening of all the supreme Jinas
has the nature of twenty aspects, and again thereafter is the very essence of many illusions.
A single moment of it bestows the fruit of equal bliss; it is not the nature of other karma.
Here, the unchanging moment that is the method is for fully entering the innate dharmadhātu.

The infinite buddha fields, the immeasurable qualities, the elements of space and the rest,
existence, called the threefold abiding, arising and perishing, and all beings in the six destinies,
the buddhas, the wrathful ones, the gods and the rest, and the bodhisattvas of compassionate mind together with their consorts:
all these are the one wheel of the single Jina, the Source of Bliss, to whom the three realms bow.

Hair and skin, flesh and blood together with fluid, and likewise bone, marrow and channels:
in these are the winds, prāṇa and the rest, and throughout, in the midst of the winds, is the course of sun and moon.

In the center of the moon, like a stainless jewel, is consciousness, embraced by all entities;
That is the very nonduality of entity and non-entity, the supreme state of nectar, the pervader, indestructible.

Hair and the rest, up to the moon, all of which abide in the body and are taught as the ten grounds,
And the six classes and the others: these at all times bestow on yogis the fruit of equal bliss.
Therefore yogis who abide at the feet of the guru should seek them for the sake of liberation.
Apart from the body there is no other buddha whatsoever, pervasive, who bestows liberation.

Sentient beings are buddhas; there is no other great buddha here in the world-realm.
By pleasing them one cuts off immeasurable existences, through nonconceptuality.
If a yogi bears malice, he will go to the far end of the great hells, such as Raurava.
Therefore, even when one's mind is pure, one should not act in conflict with people, learned or unlearned.

Having gained the company of sentient beings, O lord of men, the mind becomes pure loving-kindness and the rest.
One in saṃsāra, endowed with nonvirtuous qualities, has a mind of the six, aversion, attachment and the rest.
At all times the mind of awakening, too, is of two kinds: going, and wishing to go.
Those lowly through entering mere liberation, the reviling, those who have forsaken others: these at all times are minds of Māra.

In the abode of the three existences the māras are not peaceful, and the bodhisattvas bring peace.
The lord of māras is lowly in miraculous power, though supremely terrifying; the Teacher alone possesses miraculous power.
The māra-mind of the māras and the blissful intelligence of the protectors enter the hearts of others.
Therefore, through the powers of the buddhas, they enter the whole of the three existences for endless time.

The measure that the Jinas have stated for the world-realm is not, for human beings, gnosis itself.
For buddhas, by virtue of the innate body, there is no measure of one cubit or many;
In the land of karma, for living beings, they always make clear measures that accord with sentient beings.
If I were to state this measure according to my own wish, the gods would say, "This one is a nihilist."

That by which the Lord of Jinas is produced, by which, through the force of each day and night, he emerges from the womb;
The instant through which freedom from change is accomplished and moving becomes motionless:
Whoever abandons what is called buddha and meditates on some other emptiness, devoid of equal bliss,
For him, even through many tens of millions of eons, buddhahood and innate bliss remain far away.

Grapes do not come from the neem tree, nor nectar from poison, nor a lotus from the brahma tree;
Nor the bliss of nirvāṇa from emptiness, nor virtue from the force of nonvirtue, nor accomplishments from killing living beings;
Nor heaven for the beasts from sacrifices, nor the supremely peaceful state from suppressing the senses;
Nor the speech of the Omniscient from the Vedas, nor unchanging, motionless bliss from a changing, impure mind.

The sinful minds of sentient beings, O lord of men, come to be blessed by the hosts of Māra.
Minds of merit and gnosis, of attachment, bestowing bliss, are always blessed by the bodhisattvas.
Because, through the force of the commitment of bliss, one destroys the māras and the afflictions and goes to nirvāṇa,
Therefore the māras, at the time of each day, create many obstacles for them.

To increase this innate bliss of the Jinas, one should first rely on the action seal (karmamudrā).
Then comes the image of the sun, complete with body, face, feet, crown protuberance and all limbs,
Like a staff of lightning, she who produces nonemitting bliss, fully complete in marks and limbs,
She who shines forth through the vajras; then the body abiding in the three existences becomes the sphere of reality (dharmadhātu).

These four seals, which bestow the fruit of unchanging bliss, in towns, in wilderness retreats and charnel grounds,
In impure and pure places, in houses and in temples, everywhere and at all times,
Abiding in the joy of supreme joy, fully freed from the worldly path, engaging both the proper and the improper,
Endowed with enjoyments such as food and drink that give bodily strength and bliss, yogis should cultivate.

Through the perfect union of the winds, the tip of the lightning fire melts the moon at the crown.
Whatever drop melts from it reaches the throat and the heart, and is held at the navel and the secret place.
From the drop, the moving fluid abides in the vajra jewel; that which is held at the tip of the banner
Is the instant of wisdom-gnosis, if the garland of drops bestows bliss without emission.

Therefore, this innate instant of motionless bliss is here bestowed by the sphere of reality.
Prāṇa draws in all that abides in the flows of rasa, and having destroyed the afflictions and the māras,
Miraculous power, the ground of omniscience, and being guru of the three existences come to yogis from this very birth;
If one has fully entered the path and dies, then by holding to it, they come in another birth.

Whoever is without qualities, of little substance, and free of sense-objects: these are the two kinds of body mudrā.
Then passion, attachment, and the other treasures of qualities are the two kinds whose nature is speech.
Attached to black and to white, delighting in the qualities of sense-objects, they become the two kinds of mind mudrā,
in years of eight and two, five and five, and plainly, sky and season, and void and the Vedas.

Until the Lord of Jewels appears, the yogi should guard the supreme mother who gives birth to the Victorious.
When the Lord of Jewels fully appears, having set up the maṇḍala of the treasure of all qualities,
with the mantras of the buddhas' blessing one should emit into the secret lotus, together with the mantra of equal bliss.
Having fully guarded the secret, one should taste the bodhicitta that abides in the vajra jewel.

Among them, whoever becomes a son, that son who engenders the Victorious, is Mañjuvajra himself.
From an excess of wisdom, at times a daughter is born, and she certainly becomes a mother of the buddhas.
Then the others, from the third flower, are the Holder of the White Lotus, Jambhala, and Vajrapāṇi;
from the eighth flower come the other eight, abiding in the directions and intermediate directions, the kings of the wrathful ones, endowed with the full measure of miraculous power.

Whatever menses occurs in that month, that again fully engenders Mañjughoṣa.
For twenty years, in each menses, a bodhisattva of great courage is born;
then until the void and the Vedas, one somewhat lesser in his share of courage.
Again for twenty years, one still smaller in courage and smaller in diligence.

Then, in a womb without menses, the domain is wholly destroyed; because of that decline, there is no empowerment.
Yet to those whose seed has declined, the wisdom empowerment should be given through the word of the lord of the Victors.
Their qualities of earth, water, fire, wind, and space should be sought by means of the limbs of the Victor.
This approach of bodhicitta is what accomplishes the aims of beings, and it becomes greatly fruitful.

The bee of gods and humans that is the most excellent drinker of blood is the single nectar.
Vomit comes from the heart; and from the secret mouth, that which abides in the vajra jewel and has not entered the lotus.
Feces, urine, blood, and flesh, supremely of equal taste, have entered into the midst of the vomit.
These are the nectars of wisdom, which the guru of the three worlds has taught in all the tantras.

The bliss that has fallen into the source of phenomena of the wisdom consort should also be guarded with great effort.
Whatever sentient beings arise from it are bodhisattvas in the family of the Victors.
Therefore, at every time of day and night, the messengers of Māra, the rākṣasas, cause it to be eaten.
Eaten together with the flower of the wisdom consort, it is shown to bestow the fruit of peace and bliss.

Abiding in supreme bliss, as long as the vajra has not contracted, it should not be withdrawn from the lotus.
When desire is fulfilled, at that time this becomes blessed by the buddhas.
From the vajra and lotus it should be drawn up and placed, the wheel of gnosis set within the heart of one's own vajra;
by its rays one fills the bodies of both, and makes them possess all aspects.

If this has not been accomplished, what abides properly in the vajra jewel should be eaten.
In the place of the source of phenomena of the wisdom consort, the whole family of the Victors should be placed for the sake of protection.
This is for one endowed with a wisdom consort; then for one who is alone; and again the Buddha's body proclaimed the word.
Whoever delights in harming this, that crooked one is slain by the vajra-holder.

Whatever mantras whose nature is fierce action the lord of the three worlds has proclaimed,
all of them are for the decline of Māra's side, engendering fear there, and never for living beings.
Whoever are authors of the Smṛtis, delighting in the field of battle and in killing, and other non-Buddhists (tīrthikas) as well,
upon them the supreme sons of the Victor should apply these, for the sake of protecting living beings.

A Buddhist should not petition the ḍākinīs and the Blue Vajra ones to kill people who are like cattle.
The guru of the three worlds emanated them into the world realms for the sake of protecting sentient beings;
therefore at every time of day and night they protect, and they turn hostile toward the practitioner.
If the targets of the rite die through the force of their karma, this is the practitioner's māra.

The minds of men, through the force of the fruit of nonvirtue, are wholly attached to petty spells, thinking,
"With this spell I will bring the whole of the three worlds under my power in an instant."
With such a thought, their minds greedy, they cast aside the gift of wisdom and enter hell.
Behind them, their wealth and their own wives fall under another's power; alas, they enter into an evil dharma.

This, which is neither wisdom nor means, is the innate body, become the dharma body.
Being by nature wisdom and means, certainly free of darkness, from the distinction of gnosis and consciousness,
this is the enjoyment body, which like an echo accomplishes the aims of many beings.
For the sake of ripening beings, again this becomes the emanation body of the buddhas.

This single Vajrasattva, like the cloud at the end of time, arose as Heruka
for the ripening of the wicked; and he arose also as the Victor of the Pledge for the bliss of the deluded,
as the Lord of Jewels for those who suffer, and as the Lotus Holder for the passion of the passionate;
for the destruction of obstructors, with a sword in his lotus hand, he arose as Amoghasiddhi.

From aversion, Viśvamātā, like Agni the Crested One at the end of time, arose as the ḍākinī.
From delusion, the one called Locanā, supreme compassion; Māmakī, from the cause of pride.

From attachment comes the one called Pāṇḍarā, treasury of every quality, and Tārā from envy.
These two, of every kind of form, also arose in objects and in all the other subjects.

Through the natures of the five aggregates, out of love, he dwells in the bhagas of the vajra consorts,
for the sake of ripening the beings who have not achieved certainty and have amassed non-merit.
The Blessed One who dwells in the Pure Abodes and the rest does so for the sake of the śrāvakas;
the one who abides in the syllables evaṃ does so to set in the supreme those whose certainty is supreme.

For the sake of ripening beings, through the divisions of body, speech and mind, at all times,
the abiding of the vajra-holder truly takes three forms: outer, inner and other.
The outer becomes the various directions; in the supreme body the vital wind of prāṇa moves.
That of the All-Pervading Lord which occurs in the maṇḍalas of earth and the rest, in the bhaga of the vajra consort,

where one's own mind goes to sound and the rest, and is also without object: the other is just so.
That which abides in the syllables evaṃ, in the place of supreme bliss, is the entry of body, speech and mind.
The single vajra-holder is of three divisions, surrounded by objects, subjects and elements.
He dwells in the three places in the three times and abides in the three existences, for the sake of many beings.

What is spoken in the tantras as "Thus have I heard," I understand in this way:
the vajra-holder is the vajra arising from the melting of the moon, which in the crown, the throat, the lotus of the heart, the navel, the secret place,
and in the bhaga of women, abides in the supreme lotus through the three releases of the drop.
Having entered here into the buddha-field, it should be known by yogis as the Blessed One.

By the power of the habitual tendencies of states such as peace and passion, born from one's own heart in former births,
the one who abides in the wheel is seen as many, through the force of aspiration and qualities.
One in meaning, the speech of many languages enters the hearts of living beings according to their own natures.
For the sake of the merit of householders, abiding in the wheel, he goes forth to wander for alms.

To animals, hungry ghosts, asuras, mahoragas, gods and humans, Āryas, Tibetans and the rest,
it speaks the Dharma that is always true, in three ways: what has been, what will be, and what now is,
and through their own languages and others it sets the three existences, none left out, upon the path.
This is the speech of the Omniscient One, granting the fruit of equal bliss; it is not even the language of the gods.

Not to be fathomed even by the buddhas, endowed with immeasurable qualities, the emanation of the Buddha is an illusion;
it displays itself in the abodes of the three existences just like the bow of Śakra (Indra), the rainbow.
Divided by various things, it enters the minds of gods and humans, each their own, together with the Jinas.
This is the unborn Dharma, which, like the sky in water, gives here the illusion of being born.

The vajra of the Jina's body cannot be traversed by objects and subjects, and possesses every aspect.
The vajra of speech makes all beings understand the Dharma through the languages of their own hearts.
The vajra of the vajra-holder's mind abides on the ground that is the entire nature of beings' minds.
That which, like a stainless jewel, apprehends things: that itself is the wisdom vajra.

Generosity and the rest, granting the fruit of equal bliss, the six and the four, are declared to be the ten Powers.
Their means are those vases filled with the tenfold mind of supreme purity.
That which destroys Māra and the afflictions is called Desire; and Hara, the host of vajras, the trees and the rest;
the six mudrās are the six emptinesses; generating the three existences, wisdom is the single consciousness.

The wheel is called "the complete clarity of the three existences"; bliss is the jewel, and its very attachment
is the lotus; the exhaustion of the afflictions is the sword; and the vajras are the great wisdom body, indivisible.
Cutting through ignorance is the curved knife: these are the six supreme families, and whatever is generated by them,
the aggregates, the elements, the sense faculties and the rest, of one taste like the sky, should likewise be known.

Where the form of family has utterly come to an end, that is declared to be great form.
Where the suffering of saṃsāra has come to an end, that is declared to be great feeling.
Where the perception of saṃsāra has come to an end, that is the great vajra perception.
Where the increase of saṃsāra has come to an end, those are the vajra formations themselves.

Where the states of sleep and the rest have come to an end, that too is declared to be consciousness.
Where the state of not knowing has come to an end, that is the Sage's wisdom itself.
These, Vairocana and the rest, the supreme Jinas, are of six kinds: the six families.
The other divisions are the six elements: earth, water, fire, wind, space, and the peaceful.

Where all delusion has come to an end, that is Locanā: she is the Bearer, the earth.
Where all pride has come to an end, that is Māmakī: she is water itself.
Where all attachment has come to an end, that is Pāṇḍarā: she is fire.
Where all envy has come to an end, that is Tārā: she is wind.

Where all aversion has come to an end, that is Vajradhātvīśvarī, the Lady of the Vajra Sphere: space.
Where all avarice has come to an end, that is Viśvamātā: she is the unchanging.
That which sees forms seen and unseen, in the mind and in space as well, is the eye;
this hearing of sounds from afar as though perfectly clear is the great ear of the Jina.

105.
That which grasps every scent that brings supreme bliss is the excellent nose of the All-Pervading Lord.
That which itself constantly tastes the bliss-giving nectar of the moon is the tongue.
That which wholly captivates every vajra object of touch is always the vajra faculty of the body.
The mind that enters equal bliss through the close union of method and wisdom is its dharma.

106.
These objects and subjects, divided into six divisions, are the bodhisattvas together with their consorts.
These, form for the eye and the rest, are the six kinds of objects of the supreme Jina, wholly
clear, unborn and unceasing, bringing supreme bliss, untainted, to be realized by gnosis.
In the three realms they cannot be grasped by others, nor realized through the sense doors of any being.

107.
Here, again, Atibala summons the gnosis, and Jambhaka causes it to enter.
Stambhaka binds it, and Mānaka, through the power of supreme bliss, satisfies it.
Vajravega makes the wheels of gnosis one in taste with the wheel.
Likewise the wrathful goddesses act, clearly and definitely, in the body of the yoginī tantra.

108.
In the abodes of the three existences, Nīladaṇḍa definitely strikes the māras.
The wrathful Acala, like the sun at the end of time, binds them, and with endless vigor drives in the stake.
Sumbha makes them stand paralyzed upon the ground, their vigor sapped by desire.
Uṣṇīṣa clearly and definitely destroys their magical power, and, as before, so do the wrathful goddesses.

109.
Then, to ripen the hungry ghosts, Carcikā and the others were emanated.
To protect people, Indra and the other gods were likewise emanated in the directions and intermediate directions.
To ripen the nāgas, the vajra-holder definitely emanated the entire race of the hooded ones.
To ripen the asuras, the Great Dog-Faced Goddess and the others were also emanated.

110.
Whatever other emanations there are, down to those born of the bhūtas in the maṇḍala of the All-Pervading Lord,
these many supreme qualities, existing for the sake of all sentient beings, should be known in one's own body.
Likewise the tantras and mantras abiding in many families are divided into yoginī and yoga.
And this empowerment is twofold, worldly and world-transcending, according to the qualities of the disciple.

111.
Touching the breast of the wisdom consort, tasting the nectar of the other, and embracing,
and realizing the nectar in the fourth moment, as the bodhicitta melts in union with the wisdom consort:
all these are definitely worldly, and are taught out of supreme compassion as causes of the path.
The world-transcending empowerment is union with the supreme mudrā of the supreme Lord of the Jinas.

112.
Mere appearance of the mind, born from one's own mind, just like a reflection in a mirror,
that which all the Jina's heirs and the buddhas have relied upon is what the lord of yogins should rely upon.
Its fully blazing flame of gnosis burns up all the hosts of māras together with their objects,
and attachment to the body and the rest; and for yogins, through a year's practice, it bestows equal bliss.

113.
Wisdom and gnosis are mind and its appearances, and these become tenfold.
The empowerment is entry into this, which is like the stainless hare-marked one (the moon), like a reflection in a mirror.
From it comes the bliss of nirvāṇa, without emission, innate and unchanging: this is the fourth.
The one in whose heart and mouth this face of the Buddha dwells is the glorious guru.

114.
One whose mind is wholly fixed on the sky, whose eyes do not blink, and who has fully entered the vajra path,
will see, arising from emptiness, smoke, mirage, the clear and stainless sky-light, a lamp,
a blaze, the moon, the sun, vajras, the supreme digit, and the drop.
In their midst is the form of the Buddha, free from objects, the many bodies of perfect enjoyment.

115.
The yogin should gaze with wide-open eyes at skies free of clouds,
until a stainless black line radiating light is seen in the channel of time.
In it the form of the Omniscient One, untainted like the sun in water, of various colors,
endowed with all aspects, is one's own mind, free from objects, and no other mind.

116.
When the form has been seen, at each period of the day and night one should strive to stop the prāṇa wind,
until one sees a wheel of light rays encircling and revolving around one's own body.
For those keeping the discipline for six months, a bliss equal to the lowly bliss produced by touch becomes the mind of the path.
Abiding at the very edge between passion and dispassion, even for an instant, the count of breaths of the All-Pervading Lord increases.

117.
Between the oṭḍā and the blaze, in the middle of special joy and the innate, and of gnosis and consciousness,
at the juncture of sleep and drowsiness, whatever bliss there is in the paired union of vajra and lotus,
one should fully increase it; by the power of the guru's certainty it will increase, and in this there is no wonder.
Having vanquished the māras of the afflictions, one enters the Lord of the Jinas: through a year's practice, this is excellent yoga.

118.
Its earthly aspect is not hardness; its liquid body of water is not fluid, because it is not moist.
Its aspect of fire is not fire; its moving body of wind, since it does not move, is not wind.
Though it is the form of emptiness, it is to be seen: white, green, and of great variety of colors, yet it is not color.
Though it possesses all aspects, it is not seen, because of the power of the stains, afflictions and māras in one's own heart.

Nāda, bindu and kalā, and the channel reaching the place of the nectar of wisdom, bound like a chain;
prāṇa and apāna, the three paths, together with the vajra, the vajra joined with the lotus;
in the middle where the two winds truly meet, objects and their subjects arise and enter;
and those that grasp the signs such as smoke as well: all these, whatever they are, should be kept secret.

Make prāṇa enter fully into the central channel, and bind the course of the Sun and of the hare-marked one (the moon) on the right and the left.
Fully consorting with the mudrā, abiding in mind and in supreme bliss, even when the vajra is fully aroused,
the sound of the vajra in the lotus, or stirring it with the lotuses of one's own hands, is for the sake of bliss.
That bliss which does not emit the seed robs the fear of death: this is the word of the glorious guru.

Earth dissolves into water, water into the blazing one, and the purifying one into wind;
wind into space, and space surely becomes the ten kinds of signs, and the sign
becomes endowed with all aspects: imperishable supreme bliss, the indestructible wisdom body.
From wisdom come magical power and accomplishment, O lord of men, for persons in this very birth.

Desire surely becomes the emanation body, and completion is its speech;
blazing is the mind of emanation, and the wisdom that brings about its supreme bliss is the oṇṭā.
Joy is the enjoyment body; with supreme joy, special joy, in order, are its
speech and mind; and innate joy becomes the wisdom vajra of this Source of Bliss.

Trembling is the dharma body bowed to by the three realms of existence, and arising becomes its speech;
drowsiness is the dharma mind, and sleep is its wisdom alone, which destroys the fear of existence.
The syllable itself is the pure body; the kalā devoid of vowels, the bindu and the nāda, in order,
are the speech, mind and wisdom vajra of the pure body of the Source of Bliss, the three abiding in existence.

Waking, dream, and again the other, and the fourth: this is the nature of sleep and of the fourth.
Abiding in the body, clinging to the breath, investigating objects, and clinging to the unmoving mind;
abiding in wisdom: from fully consorting with a woman the awakening mind melts, and in its melting there is the instant.
Through the emanation body and the rest, the vajra of mind surely becomes fourfold in sequence.

Thus mind is fourfold, at the center of the drops of living beings who dwell in the three existences.
These pervaders, which grant the lords of yogins the fruit of even bliss, should be guarded for the sake of liberation.
If the drop is emitted, the supreme bliss, the seed of the yogins' birth, is gone, and what liberation is there?
Therefore those intent on restraint should at all times forsake this instant of saṃsāric bliss.

Because the vowels beginning with a, the consonants beginning with ka, the hare-marked one and the day-maker alone are not the seat of the vajra-holder,
and other colors and forms, transformed by the syllable hūṃ itself together with the sign, are not desired,
that which is produced by the unchanging, in which change has ceased, and which is endowed with the supreme faculties,
belongs to this one endowed with all aspects, all the drops, the Lord of the Jinas who holds the manifold illusion.

The eight vowels beginning with ḷ, which become the qualities of objects, the short ones with half-vowels,
divided by mutual division, fifty-six and a quarter: so flow the breaths of the central channel.
In the second period, by force of the qualities of objects, the long ones, divided by mutual division;
and again in the third watch, the very long ones, the qualities of objects: so flow the breaths of the central channel.

Thus in the four watches, six hundred and seventy-five are the breath-vowels of the central channel.
The letters beginning with ka, thirty, through their division into thirty, are clearly nine hundred.
Divided by the division of the six vowels, five thousand four hundred, in the three channels of the three periods;
likewise those divided by the long ones, in the course of the latter part of the day, in the three channels of the three periods.

Again, those divided by the six vowels, at night, in the three channels of the three periods;
likewise those divided by the long ones, in the course of the latter part of the night, in the three channels of the three periods.
In each day and night, the breaths that go out and come in flow twenty-one thousand six hundred times.
Having gathered the former empty ones, one should apply them to the central channels in the even and uneven courses.

Outwardly, that which is half of the eighth division of the water-clock periods abides apart from the enjoyments of Rāhu.
In one's own body it is surely half a breath, free from darkness, illuminating all the worlds;
the former half is with darkness, in which the three existences all dissolve into the empty ones.
Therefore, piercing it, the yogin of Kālacakra alone enters, free from darkness.

That supreme portion of the All-Pervading Lord, less than the moon and more than the Sun, here in the four watches,
moving in watch after watch by force of day and night, through the division into half-periods,
in the division of thirty-six, the messengers of time, doubled; the other lords of men, in the division of the lunar days, twice eight;
in the division of the planets, eight; and again many others, in the groups of the junctures.

The remainder of the breaths of the four times are the ḍākinīs of manifold form who produce the three existences.
That which is deficient in the center of the moon becomes the twofold mudrā of the supreme Lord of the Jinas himself.
That which abides in the Sun is the eightfold division, on the petals of the directions and intermediate directions, the end of the enjoyment of the watches.
The remainder are the supremely terrifying goddesses of the wheel; all the others are outside and in the body.

Saturn, the apertures, the dark one: this is called a day; with half of Jupiter, the guru of the gods, eyes and the number of eyes,
With half, six and the Vedas they become; these are born from the vajra mind of the supreme Lord of the Jinas.
Mars, emptiness, serpent, moon, sun, Mercury, Venus, the comet, and again emptiness, serpent, sky, one,
Sky, eyes, eyes, and one: these arise from the pure speech of the supreme All-Pervading Lord.

That which is the enjoyment of the moon's time, sky, sky, tastes, fire: these arise from the vajra of body.
All become one; tastes, six, serpent and seasons are pure, and so too the half water-clock period.
The ḍākinīs that have the form of time all dwell in the body and steal the prāṇa of living beings.
Therefore, at the time of each day, one should block their path and accomplish them.

As long as the planets' enjoyment abides in their own and others' passage, through the movement of inhalation and exhalation,
For that long, how could the pervading messenger of time bestow the supreme in the three realms?
The subtle messenger moving freely, abiding in the guru's word, in the awakening mind and the immutable,
Is to be known by those free of evil and endowed with yoga; it is not otherwise known by a mind of desire.

On the feet of the sun and the moon, through the force of the passage of both, those multiplied by craving
Are divided by the limbs; the remaining foot is the comet's, which devours at Rāhu's entry.
At all the junctures of the watches and in the eight watches, at the time of each day, the twelve limbs of living beings,
At each conjunction of time, the breaths as well: the limb is declared to be the flowing of twice nine hundred prāṇas.

For each pair of limbs, by the force of the first day, ignorance is first, on to the twelfth limb.
Therefore the limbs are by time and the rest; the other, the ninth, is whatever was formerly other.
Thus by these five kinds, in time as well, everywhere it comes about from each pair of months.
Where one digit of the moon has become diminished, that alone is the first month.

By six months and three times they are grasped through the twelve limbs of dependent origination.
The sixth, a division of the sun's time, by the force of the feet's passage, enters into the middle of the moon.
In that middle, the limb of contact, Rāhu seizes the foot of the moon and the sun.
Whatever medicines Rāhu has gazed upon at the times of day and night grant accomplishment.

Mars, Venus, Mercury, moon, sun, Mercury, Venus, Mars, Jupiter, Saturn, Saturn,
Jupiter: in the houses of Aries and the rest, at the time of each day, these are the house lords, at all times.
For pacifying, the houses of the moon and the sun; the houses of Mercury, Venus, Mars, Jupiter and Saturn are for increasing,
For subjugating, summoning, separating and killing; they become supreme if here they are the house lord.

For pacifying, the white face, in the first; for increasing, one like white and yellow, in the watch of the hand;
For immobilizing, the yellow, in the watch of fire; for stupefying, one like gold and the sun, in that of the oceans; for summoning,
The red, arising from the fifth; for subjugating, one like the sun and the ocean, in the sixth watch;
For killing, the black face, in that of the mountain; for expelling, black and white, here in the serpent, at the end of night.

Earth, water, fire, wind, the hare-marked one, the sun, and the god: these are not the ones who enjoy prāṇa.
These are not the enjoyers of prāṇa either; through the force of the fruit of nonvirtue the self imagines that it enjoys.
The host of bhūtas, clinging to craving and suffering, make their petitions to the planets, the hand-goers and the gods.
That by whose kindness one becomes liberated, peace through the mind, is not an agent.

The first breath is of a nature without qualities; by the force of the three qualities it becomes three in number.
That too, by the force of the qualities of the objects, becomes what is called the lunar day; these again tripled
Are multiplied by the number of the elements and the oceans; then those, multiplied in turn by the divisions of movement,
Are the numbers of breaths of a half water-clock period; doubled again, they are the numbers of breaths of a water-clock period.

By two, three, oceans, arrows, seasons, mountains, eight, planets and ten this root of time increases;
Then, through the divisions of the classes, the root of time abiding on the twofold path
Completely fills the three worlds; from the full movement of the moon and sun, which bestow results of happiness and evenness,
As far as Vedas, serpent and fire, it surely comes to bear a curved knife in hand and a skull cup.

Wheels, arrows and oceans in number, by the force of objects and qualities, through the divisions of body, speech and mind,
Guard the Principal One and bestow results of happiness and evenness: these are the eight goddesses, Dhūmā and the rest.
Thus the Principal One abiding in the sun, the supreme, embraced by the digit of the hare-marked one, of various forms,
Is worthy of homage from goddesses, buddhas and immortals, from objects and subjects, from Rāhu, the moon and the sun.

The subtle essence of the Principal One, the supreme qualities and objects, whatever mudrās there are, inner and outer,
These in the three realms are the ḍākinīs born from the supremely terrifying mind of the wrathful ones.
Whatever faculties of the Principal One there are, abiding in threefold existence, the vajras of body, speech and mind,
These, embraced by the qualities together with their own objects, are these vajra ḍākas, beginning with the Auspicious One.

Form, sound, taste and smell, and the other as well, then touch, smoke and dust,
And sattva in the mind, are successively made manifest, and are likewise embraced by Rāhu too.
From their movements, slow and six-footed, swift, crooked and the rest, comes this manifold passage:
On the six feet of the eight, the eight objects and qualities are rightly placed in the manner of a wheel.

The first breath has eight divisions; abiding in objects and qualities, it is surrounded on all sides by the ḍākinīs,
Dhūmā and the rest, and by the force of the threefold passage it wanders and abides in the six destinies.
In its center is the pure body of Kālacakra, embraced by the digit that radiates nectar.
Whoever cuts off the breath and enters the body of the Jina, that one alone is Kālacakra.

At the end, when the breath is cut off, there is neither this threefold course nor the sixfold one.
When the drop is covered, one enters wholly into the supreme All-Pervading Lord, endless bliss, at that time.
Having abandoned the pleasures of saṃsāra, the yogin should meditate on the face of the supreme guru.
The yoginīs take no delight in conduct of seductive forms or in the supremely terrifying.

In the breath of the Lord, all the feet of the host of planets dissolve through the conjunctions of time.
When the breaths have gone through sky, sky, space, sky, eyes, fire and the oceans, all is impelled into emptiness.
Through these years, outwardly, in the nature of the emanation and withdrawal of the feet of the supreme All-Pervading Lord,
one should know, each by its own measure, the going and abiding of the feet of the Lord of the Jinas in the midst of the breath.

Saturn's entry into the void comes, by the force of its own transit through its days, to the end of time;
with the remaining enjoyments of the days of void, space, sky, lakes, oceans and moon, it is Revatī.
With the days of its own transit, sky, sky, space, sky, lakes and eyes, Rāhu enters there.
With the days of its own transit, sky, sky, space, sky and seasons, the minister of the god of time enters.

With the days of space, sky, void, sky, eight, serpents and eyes, Maṅgala enters;
with days numbering sky, sky, space, sky, eyes, fire and the oceans, the Sun and the rest enter.
With the days of sky, sky, space, sky, lakes, eight, moon and arrows, the Moon enters there;
at that time the courses of the kings decline completely, each by its own measure.

Then, at the rising of moon and sun as well, this circulation of the channels takes place.
One, two, three, oceans, arrows, tastes, mountains and vasus increasing: these are the secret channels.
The aperture becomes the channel of the door; the tenth, the subtle channel, goes again to the upper path.
These, divided by classes, with their qualities and with their objects, become the channels of the wheels.

The direction of the crown and the heart, and the head, the abode of the hare-marked one, the direction of the throat, the course of the Sun;
at the navel, the channels of the wheel are seasons, hand-goers, lords of men, thirty-two, and their double.
They are not distinct through the feet of the moon, nor distinct even when mixed with all the feet of the sun.
The hundred and eighty-one that abide in hands and feet, the channels of the joints, are the divisions of the two.

In the direction of the crown and the heart are the feet of the hare-marked one and of the Sun; the rest are in the wheel of the throat.
The eighty-one, the last, the radiant tenfold: these are the peaceful and the fierce planets.
Their portions of beginning and end, abiding at the feet of the All-Pervading Lord, are enjoyed by the channel of time,
and the end of the planets is enjoyed by the ḍākinī of vajra body, the mother of the three existences.

She who first is of the nature of emptiness, abiding in objects and qualities, becomes their intrinsic nature;
she fully enters the elements and aggregates, and again is equal in the eye and the other faculties;
she enters waking, dream and the rest, and truly and fully enters supreme equal bliss as indestructible.
That knowledge consort, the Mother of the Buddhas, abiding in the vajra joints, the yogin should seek.

From the power of the Lord's body, the divine eye arises in the supreme body of the yogins.
From the power of his speech, the ear; from his mind arises knowledge of what abides in the hearts of others.
From the power of the wisdom body arises recollection of former births abiding in the threefold existence.
From the power of the wisdom speech arises the miraculous power of going everywhere, equal to space, at all times.

From the nature of wisdom and gnosis, that which is equal bliss becomes indestructible at all times.
From the power of the divine eye, one sees what the eye has not seen, and the threefold existence too.
From the power of the divine ear, one hears the sounds of heart and mouth uttered by living beings.
Likewise, from the union of body, speech and mind, O lord of men, everything, touch and the rest, comes about.

The channels which, in the middle of apāna, are the threefold path, abiding, through which urine, feces and semen flow:
the coursing of prāṇa in these becomes ninefold, the last being the face of the Buddha.
In the ears, nose and eyes it is known as twofold; the uvula together with the tongue;
and the drop becomes the aperture of the crown, the tenth, which is sealed for those who do not know.

When prāṇa and apāna are stopped, the hare-bearer, set in motion, becomes the form of the Sun.
When awakened by vajra and lotus, this is again melted by the flames of the Sun's form.
When wind, moon and sun are stopped, consciousness and gnosis become one.
Thus, thus, and likewise thus, it becomes threefold; there is nothing else at all.
When the forms of moon and sun are consumed, in the sky there is never either day or night.
That juncture, in the middle of the body, is for yogins at all times abiding in the state of nectar.
Just as the waning moon abiding in the sky becomes of one taste with the Sun's form,
so when prāṇa and apāna are exhausted, clearly in the body, at the time of accomplishment, that ceases.

When the courses of moon and sun have declined, and in both paths as well, the great wind of prāṇa ceases.
The lotus and the vajra are fully awakened, the hare-marked one melts and fully enters the great form of the sun.
Entity and non-entity become one, and the indestructible abiding in threefold existence is well and fully realized.
At that time the yogin, in whom there is not the slightest duality, goes to the supreme state.

The wrathful ones, the buddhas together with the goddesses, and indeed the vajra goddesses of taste, Kṣitigarbha and the others,
with minds full of joy, become directly visible in the sky to the yogin at the time of accomplishment.
His desired accomplishment comes at dawn or at midnight of the lunar day of the hare-marked one.
Through union with the time that has become supreme, a rain of jewels or of flowers falls upon the earth.

The body is clear, its particles dissolved, equal to space, fully perfected with the major marks and the rest.
The variegated three worlds themselves appear clear, free of obscuration, like a dream.
Speech is wholly without interruption, and in many other languages it enters the hearts of others.
The mind, which supreme bliss leaves unmoving, is at all times embraced by the innate.

From earth, the particles of water, Agni the Crested One, and wind are separated, and it enters the element of water.
From water, earth, fire, and wind are separated, and it enters Agni the Crested One. From Agni the Crested One, earth, water, and wind are separated,
and it enters wind. From wind, earth, water, and Agni the Crested One are separated, and the karmic wind too enters space.
Those others struck by the karmic wind through the power of threefold existence go to other fields.

Having beheld all the buddha-fields, as those who bring forth the three existences free of karma-bound beings,
on the pure particles of the equal taste of both, all the buddhas are surrounded by bodhisattvas.
On each individual particle of taste, through the power of the family of both, they are surrounded by the vajra sons.
On the particles of earth and the rest, those abiding in all the families, who have fully entered Ananta, Acala, and the others,

together with them, abide in space until the time when Vajrasattva takes form.
By the act of holding firmly, the particles whose nature is earth, water, the eater of offerings, wind, and space are held.
By the act of churning, the distinct particles are churned until all of them become one.
Then once again they are joined with one another: the lesser entities to the principal entity.

All the great winds of shape arrange all the buddha-fields without exception.
Again, in each individual world-system, the great encircling mountains stand all around.
In their center the great lands of karma, by their nature, become the world-system.
The rest, by nature lands of enjoyment, bringing the pleasure of sense-objects, are filled with every kind of jewel.

Of the lord of mountains, the east is wholly pure sapphire, the south is ruby,
the west yellow katana gem, the north white like the hare-bearer, moonstone crystal.
The center is green. At its heart, contained within it, here is the great maṇḍala:
first, the nature of mind; then, from the middle, twice that, the nature of speech.

Then the other is twice that again: thousands of yojanas, the arrows multiplied by ten.
Outside it, by the seasons, is the essence of the wheel, and then the nature of the world-system.
With the six wheels and the six classes of beings, a hundred thousand times the Vedas, it abides on the maṇḍala of wind: this is the maṇḍala.
Twice eight pillars; by the lords of men, the digits of the hare-marked one, a storied house, round, in the form of the source of birth.

From the crown protuberance of the Lord of the Jinas to the center of the ūrṇā hair is a sun and a half of finger-widths.
Then the lotus of the throat, likewise the heart, and then the navel and the lotus of the secret place, likewise.
Below the feet, the shins, the thighs, and the hips: Manu, the principles, the principles, and the Vedas.
Half the chest is a sun and a half; one's own shoulders and arms are by the measure of space, eyes, kings, and suns.

That which is a hundred exceeded by some twenty; by the seasons and nine, the measure of the world of humans.
By Vedas with a half, four, the Lord of the Waters, the Lord of the Waters with a half, Vedas, and Vedas,
come the crown protuberance, the head below it, the glorious forehead of the Lord of the Jinas, and the nose,
below the nose to the end of the chin, then the throat, and the center of the lotus at the root of the throat.

Then the heart, navel, and secret place come in sequence by the lords of men and by suns with a half.
The secret lotus, the vajras too at the root of the navel, and by the sages the crown protuberance above.
The ūrṇā hair, and in the center of the secret lotus, where the drop of semen abides, producing unchanging bliss:
this glorious vajra ground, through the power of the family of both, is in the wheels of body, speech, and mind.

A sun and a half, twenty-five, and twice that again: the wheels of mind, speech, and body.
Half of the mind is six finger-widths with a quarter, abiding at the heart: the wheel of innate wisdom.
From the three the doors are made, adorned with the nature of jewels, an eighth part of the maṇḍalas.
Through going and coming, above and below, the wheels of body, speech, and mind become doubled.

For the one abiding on Meru as well, half a finger-width of the Lord of the Jinas becomes thousands of yojanas.
Likewise the mountain of the gods becomes the measure of the body, and its breadth is the measure of half.
Above it the vajra form is stacked, a third part, and half of that again.
Likewise below are earth, water, fire, and wind, with the flames: the vajra garland.

By a sun and a half comes the wheel of mind of the Lord of the Jinas: the navel, and the center of the throat lotus.
The wheel of speech and the wheel of body, from the place of the vajra jewel upward to the boundary at half the ūrṇā hair.
At the end of the door-portico come the glorious cheeks; above the forehead, by the lords of men, the boundary of the crown protuberance.
By suns with a half come the maṇḍalas of earth, water, fire, and wind, with the flames: the vajra garland.

Above and below, this buddha body becomes equal: the wheels of body, speech, and mind.

Through the power of the wind's movement all around, by the measure across, by threes, or likewise by sixes,
With eight spokes and emblems, and with the feet of the Lord of the Jinas, it becomes a wheel by six finger-widths.
It is surrounded by eight gates, and with the palm of one's own hand and with the nails the glorious charnel grounds should be determined.

Above and below, at the face and at the secret place, by measures of two and of four, they become the maṇḍalas of the Blessed Lady.
At half the lotus, by threes from the center of the heart, and from there, six wheels with garlands;
The gates and the rest, and the wheels on the sides, all around, certainly come about by fifths.
The innate body, this form of the stūpa, is in every respect neither wisdom nor method.

Outside Meru, in the region of the lower ground, the eight accomplishments abide:
In the east is the sword; then the elixir, the nectar fruit, the pill, the eye ointment, and rocanā;
In the south, glorious swift-footedness; all the inexhaustible treasures are in the fire corner of the maṇḍalas.
In the center are the things borne aloft for worship; that is the very Brahmā-voice of the teacher of gods and humans.

The jewel at the crown, the forehead, the place of the heart, the navel and the secret place, and the vajra
Measure one, two, four, five, eight, nine, and twelve finger-widths, in order.
Center, upper and lower are its three parts; closed and spread apart, for peaceful and for fierce rites.
The bells likewise have three parts: the top like the vajra, with the shape of a cow's mouth and of a lotus.

The hub with its face is a third of its extent, and the petals are a third of that;
The prongs equal the extent, in the directions and intermediate directions, and the rounded part of the prongs is a quarter.
The outer prongs are lower by a third, and on top the shape of a jujube thorn is joined.
The mouths of the bells are likewise; the petals and the vajra as well; the cow's mouth and the rest are just the same.

By the span from the secret lotus to the edge of the ūrṇā is the measure of the height of the stūpa of the supreme Lord of the Jinas.
By half of that is the breadth of the seat, perfectly level like the ground, and by a third of that, the side portions.
At the ends it projects outward by one and a half; the circle where the lotus reaches the heart is round, by the measure of the seat.
From the edge of the lotus, a lesser portion equal to the base is the chest, and by its side portion, the neck.

By a portion of half the seat is the face, perfectly level like the ground, and by a third of that, the side portions.
From the secret lotus, twelve and a half in height is the measure of the perfectly level seat;
From the seat, the boundary of the lotus, the edge of the circle reaching the heart, and then the base of the neck;
Then to the end of the face, likewise, by four, six, and four and a half: three sections.

From the seat, by fours, the lotus, the circle reaching the heart, and the measure of the neck likewise;
By eight and a half, the measure of the face, and above it comes the row of parasols, O lord of men.
From the boundary of the ūrṇā come the forehead and the head, and from there it reaches here to the end of the uṣṇīṣa.
Thus, certainly, the entire world-system is the body of the Jina, having the nature of the wheel and the stūpa.

Gold becomes the earth element, silver water, and copper is the fire element;
Iron is wind, and these, tin and lead, have the nature of elements mixed with space.
Yellow gems become earth, white ones water, and red gems are fire;
Black ones are wind; blue and green likewise arise from mixed elements.

Salt becomes earth, sweet becomes water, pungent is fire, and bitter is wind;
Sour and astringent tastes are mixed. Then again, the sources of the crops of the fields,
Medicines of the six tastes and the supreme taste, the other elements, the lords of gems,
The various tangible things on the earth, and the waters that remove all disease, are truly so.

Earth removes stabbing pain, and the water element removes poison; fire
Removes the bhūtas, wind removes stiffness, and space removes the stroke of the evil eye.
Gnosis removes everything; likewise also the gems worn on the bodies of kings.
The powers of waters, tastes, gems, touch, mantras and medicines are all inconceivable.

The Blessed One, whose nature is the wheel of gnosis, enters into the buddhafields everywhere,
And again, from the joining of all the assemblages, by the force of results, sentient beings enter in.
By the force of their own daily course, through those twelve limbs, here where the moon and Meru are complete,
The stūpa form stacked upward, of vajra nature, O lord of men, is its heart maṇḍala.

Outside are the wheels of the lunar mansions; on the ground of action, humans dwell upon the earth;
On the ground of enjoyment are the six continents, and others, gods and the rest, whose food is the taste of nectar fruits.
Dwelling at the corners are the eight: the lord of the gods (Indra), Agni, Kāla (Yama), the asura, the Lord of the Waters, Vāyu,
