The alchemical verses (from "The combustible one is earth" onward) use specialized materia-medica terms whose exact identifications are uncertain. "The combustible one," "the moving one" (likely mercury), "zetsa salt," "the three curved knives," "the thorn-born" iron, "the black heap" (likely kālakūṭa poison) and "transmutes by the spear" are rendered literally. The correlation of minerals with the elements in the line on kākṣī, kāśīsa, sulfur, realgar and orpiment is an interpretation of compressed syntax. "Single caste" refers to the Kalki's unification of the sages into one vajra family. "The Lord of the World" is the speaker's self-designation.  This passage belongs to the Kālacakra's treatment of alchemy (rasāyana), where "elixir" (rasa) refers chiefly to mercury. Several mineral and technical terms are uncertain. "Pyrite" renders lhang tsher, taken as mākṣika. "Realgar" for gsha' tshe is a tentative identification. "Monkey fat" for spra tshil is literal. "Swing apparatus" for khyogs is taken as the dolāyantra. "Placement" for dgod pa, "the double salt," "the great one," and "without an opening" (kha med) are literal renderings of technical terms whose precise referents are unclear. The "vessel of the moon" and "the Sun and the hare-marked one" are taken as silver and gold, following alchemical usage.  This passage is alchemical (rasāyana and metal transmutation), and many mineral and plant terms are uncertain. "Sharp gold," "nāga," "the moon" (probably silver or camphor), "the sky" (probably mica, abhra), gśa' tshe, lhang tsher skyes, dmar ring and pokkāṇa are given literally or in transliteration because their exact identity is unclear. "Drawn out by the vowels" is literal; the vowels may stand for the number sixteen. "A month together with the lord of men" may be a numerical code, since "lord of men" (nṛpa) can stand for sixteen. "Those called fields upon the earth" may refer to the guardians of the sacred fields (kṣetrapālas). The phrase translated "overpower violently" literally reads "give fierce victory." Here དབུ་མ is the central channel (madhyamā), not the Madhyamaka school.  Verses 235 to 242 use the Indian convention of number words, where objects stand for numbers (directions for ten, apertures for nine, the twice-born, meaning teeth, for thirty-two); these have been translated literally. In the verse beginning "Whoever in the world of humans," the clause rendered "to rage at him with the fire of wrath is what is called the great wrath" is syntactically uncertain in the Tibetan. "O Sucandra" takes ཟླ་བ་བཟང as an address to the king to whom the tantra was taught.  "At the time of Ananta" renders མཐའ་ཡས་དུས; it may instead mean "at the endless time." Its exact technical sense in the Kālacakra yoga of the winds is uncertain.

"Candra's request" refers to the request of King Sucandra, who received the root tantra.

The "tantra of few vowels and consonants" is the condensed tantra, the Laghutantra. Its speaker here is Kulika Yaśas, addressing the sage Sūrya ("the Sun") and his son Puṇḍarīka.

In the verse beginning "O Sun, you will be a lord of men," the syntax is compressed, and the reading of the first line, and of "the one with the white lotus" as Puṇḍarīka identified with Lokanātha (Avalokiteśvara), is somewhat uncertain.

ས་ག is rendered as the month of Vaiśākha.

The commentary Puṇḍarīka is told to compose is traditionally the Vimalaprabhā.

The Dharma king in the colophon is not named in the text.

This is an AI-generated translation from the AI Stupa project. It was produced by a careful, doctrine-aware process aiming at a high degree of accuracy, but it has not been reviewed by a human translator and may contain errors. It is offered as a provisional translation for those texts of the Tibetan Buddhist canon that have not yet received an authoritative human translation. Where a published translation exists — above all from 84000: Translating the Words of the Buddha (84000.co) — that translation is definitive and should be preferred; please consult it instead. These translations are released into the public domain (CC0) in the spirit of spreading the Dharma to all corners of the earth.

Just as the Bodhi tree now standing at Bodh Gayā neither is nor is not the same tree beneath which the Buddha attained awakening, so too this stupa is intended as the next generation of stupa — dependently arising according to the conditions of its environment, and ceaselessly proclaiming the Dharma for the benefit of all sentient beings.

May whoever encounters these words derive great benefit.
